# The 128 GB Club: model comparison

Generated by `tools/model_compare.py render` on 2026-11-15 from `models/*.json`. **Do not edit by hand**: change the JSON files, then run the command again.

Facts are verifiable: each one has its source and the date it was checked, in the model's section. "Fits 128 GB" follows the definition in `research-method.md` (Memory target). Appraisals summarise web feedback in our own words, with links. "Positive" and "Negative" count the web comments of each kind; each count links to the model's section, where every comment is listed with its source link and date.

## Details

### DeepSeek V4-Flash-0731

File: [`models/deepseek-v4-flash-0731.json`](models/deepseek-v4-flash-0731.json).

Sources: huggingface `deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731` [facts] – official weights, 167 GB, too large; huggingface `antirez/deepseek-v4-gguf` – 2-bit, 86.7 GB, plus MTP file 3.8 GB, for DwarfStar; huggingface `antirez/deepseek-v4-gguf` – mixed 2/4-bit, 97.6 GB, for DwarfStar; huggingface `unsloth/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-GGUF` – UD-IQ2_M, 90.9 GB, for llama.cpp.

| runtimes | DwarfStar (ds4, Metal-first, own GGUF files, beta, 96 GB+); llama.cpp llama-server with GGUF (ggml-org, unsloth UD-IQ2_M); LM Studio (lmstudio-community GGUF); MLX low-bit builds in mlx-community (2.4bit-mixed, OptiQ-2bit). Ollama: official library has only cloud tags (V4.1 Flash, V4 Pro); local only via community frob/deepseek-v4-flash-0731 (DwarfStar README names V4 Flash and a Flash 0731 checkpoint but fetches its own files with download_model.sh; that antirez/deepseek-v4-gguf is the same source is unverified. Whether the community Ollama tag runs well locally, and whether stock mlx-lm loads the 0731 MLX builds, are unverified. A newer V4.1-Flash-1017 exists on HF (ggml-org, mlx-community, unsloth).) | [github.com/antirez/ds4](https://github.com/antirez/ds4) (README), [huggingface.co/ggml-org/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-…](https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-GGUF), [huggingface.co/unsloth/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0…](https://huggingface.co/unsloth/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-GGUF), [huggingface.co/lmstudio-community/DeepSeek…](https://huggingface.co/lmstudio-community/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-GGUF), [huggingface.co/mlx-community/DeepSeek-V4-F…](https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-2.4bit-mixed), [ollama.com/search?q=deepseek-v4](https://ollama.com/search?q=deepseek-v4) | 2026-11-14 |
| fits_128gb | yes (largest file set ≤ 100 GB) | see size_gb | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): The strongest model that fits on 128 GB according to many Mac users, at the cost of a 2-bit quant and most of the memory. Test it against Qwen3.8-27B.

**Negative**

- A 128 GB Mac only just fits it ([pornel, 16.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48563585)); 128 GB is too little for a good quantisation ([cmrdporcupine, 23.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49020516)).
- Still produces a malformed tool call from time to time ([kamranjon, 14.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48913036)); a lot of fan noise on a MacBook Pro M5 Max ([reverius42, 31.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49120467)).
- An M5 Max 128 GB Mac Studio owner settled on Qwen models instead ([fzxu22, 05.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50385925)).
- No vision in the 2-bit text build ([kamranjon, 12.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48503585)).

**Positive**

- Used as the main model for everything on an M4 Max 128 GB MacBook Pro with DwarfStar ([kamranjon, 12.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48503585)).
- ~24 tok/s generation, ~200 tok/s prefill on an M4 Max 128 GB; better at long tool-calling tasks than GPT-4-class models ([jumploops, 13.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48510866)).
- Pretty good speed and coding ability on a Mac Studio 128 GB ([davedx, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50148812)).
- Even at 2-bit, among the best local models one user has benchmarked ([spottedmarley, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49298258)); results far better than usual for a 2-bit quantisation ([Catloafdev, 17.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49741914)).

Strata's own measurement on an M5 Max 128 GB (2.00 bpw, build 0.2.7.1, another model loaded at the same time): 15–18 tok/s on a short question, a 3,000-token prompt in about 11 s, about 68 GB of experts kept in memory; context above 32K not measured (checked 15.11.2026)) | [github.com/antirez/ds4/blob/main/docs/MODE…](https://github.com/antirez/ds4/blob/main/docs/MODELS.md), [github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/31086](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/31086), [unsloth.ai/docs/models/deepseek-v4.1-flash](https://unsloth.ai/docs/models/deepseek-v4.1-flash), [github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/docs/…](https://github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/docs/DEEPSEEK_MAC.md) | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-14, feedback thin): Users find it far better than V4 Flash and close to frontier models on well-defined tasks, but nearly all of that is API use. Local reports on 128 GB Macs are few: usable but behind Codex and Claude, coherent at 1 bit, some looping at 2 bit. No local report on tool calling.

**Negative**

- Communicates less well than Google models, API use ([bscphil, 10.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50442722)). Garbled replies in non-thinking mode ([HF #33](https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017/discussions/33)) and overthinking ([HF #29](https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017/discussions/29)).

**Positive**

- Feels much better than V4 Flash ([liuliu, 07.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50405607)); far better than V4, with about twice the parameters, and on par with Opus 5.5 on well-defined tasks, local or API not stated ([sjbzbeiks, 07.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50408642)).
- Found a community Ollama build of 0731 almost useless; very happy with the Unsloth GGUF of 1017 for coding, quantisation and hardware not stated ([ericpruitt, 07.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50409154)).

**Neutral**

- Tested V4 and V4.1 Flash locally on an M5 Mac Studio with 128 GB: both useful, but they need steering and retries and are well behind Codex and Claude ([davedx, 12.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50470346)).
- An IQ1 quant was coherent and of good quality once the runtime settings were fixed; a reply reports looping with a Q2 build ([latentsea and ferroman, 09–11.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50422422)).
- Runs on 128 GB and more with Unsloth's SSD offloading, no figures ([selcuka, 06.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50386477)). V4.1-Flash (before 1017) at 4 bits streamed from SSD on an M5 Max 128 GB: about 14 tok/s decode, from a self-promoting source ([Argonautlabs, 15.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49712330)).
- MLX on an M3 Ultra 256 GB uses about 204 GB of memory ([HF #78](https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash-1017/discussions/78)); Q4 SSD-streaming decode about 8–9 tok/s on a 256 GB M3 Ultra ([ds4 #1085, 18.09.2026](https://github.com/antirez/ds4/issues/1085)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): Well regarded as an API model, close to Sonnet for some users. Locally it only fits on 128 GB at 1–2 bits, with less margin than DeepSeek V4-Flash. Thin local feedback.

**Negative**

- Compared with GLM-5.3/flash, Sonnet seems lazier but follows prompts better ([disgruntledphd2, 12.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50468259)).

**Positive**

- Flash models such as GLM 5.3 Flash and DS 4.1 Flash 1017 judged very good, very fast and very cheap ([rheisen_, 12.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50470173)).
- Little difference found between Sonnet and GLM-5.3/flash ([disgruntledphd2, 12.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50468259)).
- Used together with DeepSeek V4.1 Flash for cross-validation ([joehuangsweden, 13.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50476424)).
- Listed with DeepSeek V4 Flash among the large MoE models for 128 GB machines: 320B parameters, 18B active ([Tepix, 26.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49454403)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Good for its time, but by late 2026 users find it weaker than Qwen3.8-27B, especially for code and tool use.

**Negative**

- Outperformed by newer, smaller models: most recent 27B models beat it ([Art9681, 25.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49436170)); Qwen3.8 27B is clearly better at coding ([kennywinker, 12.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49676994)); only 5B active parameters and ≈ 13 months older than Qwen3.8 ([ac29, 12.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49678007)).
- Mediocre results and clear underperformance in one user's own evaluations ([shard972, 24.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49825770)).
- Prone to overthinking: 3–10k tokens of reasoning per turn ([nostrebored, 03.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49556894)).
- Tool calls announced but never made, thinking blocks never closed, loops ([LoganDark, 19.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49359217), [24.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49829591)). Caveat: this was on Cerebras, a cloud provider; it may not apply to Ollama.

**Positive**

- Still very useful ([0xbadcafebee, 19.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49362704)); close to the frontier when it was released ([Philpax, 28.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49481096)).
- Answered a simple reasoning question correctly, like other models ([epihelix, 25.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49840038)).
- Still chosen by a maker of 128 GB local-AI boxes as the larger model for harder tasks ([moezee1, 28.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49873613)).

### Kimi K3 and K3.1 Pro (Moonshot AI)

File: [`models/kimi-k3.json`](models/kimi-k3.json).

Whether stock mlx-lm loads the REAP build is inferred from the mlx-community conversion.) | [huggingface.co/unsloth/Kimi-K3-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/unsloth/Kimi-K3-GGUF), [github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/28126](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/28126), [huggingface.co/mlx-community/Kimi-K3-mlx-r…](https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/Kimi-K3-mlx-reap160-2bit), [ollama.com/library/kimi-k3/tags](https://ollama.com/library/kimi-k3/tags) | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Several HN users moved daily coding to K3 or K3.1 Pro through the API and place it just below Opus 5.5; one heavy user finds it clearly stronger than DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, another finds K3.1 a bit behind it. Locally on a 128 GB M5 Max, SSD streaming gives about 1 tok/s (K3) and 2 tok/s (K3.1): batch jobs only. An 80 % pruned MLX build reaches 5.5 tok/s but needs 512 GB and loses quality; low-bit Unsloth GGUFs had loader and corrupted-output bugs. K3.1 complaints: a hard-wired persona that ignores system prompts, and cyber data removed from training. The $20M model-as-a-service clause is criticised.

**Negative**

- **K3.** WASTE engine: full K3 on a 64 GB M5 Pro at about 0.32 tok/s, very sensitive to memory pressure (0.03–0.46). A reviewer found its VQ3R expert quantisation has about 19.4 % weight error, which they see as a deliberate weakening. Metal 22 % slower than CPU. ([marcobambini, HF #148, 2026-07-29](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K3/discussions/148))
- **K3.** REAP80 MLX mxfp4-q8 build (80 % of experts pruned, about 350 GB): about 5.54 tok/s on a 512 GiB M3 Ultra; its own card warns of noticeable degradation (drift, repetition loops, weak Chinese), code structurally correct; no benchmarks against the full model. ([pipenetwork model card, 2026-07-28](https://huggingface.co/pipenetwork/Kimi-K3-REAP80-MLX-mxfp4-q8))
- **K3.** Low-bit GGUF problems: UD-TQ2_0 loads on 768 GB of GPUs but logits are corrupted; the Q1/TQ2 files use ggml types 64–66, rejected by mainline llama.cpp. A llama.cpp RPC run on Apple Silicon crashed after 430 GB of UD-Q2_K_XL (closed as stale). ([bperin42, unsloth HF #18, 2026-08-11](https://huggingface.co/unsloth/Kimi-K3-GGUF/discussions/18); [llama.cpp #28126, 2026-08-31](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/28126))
- **K3.** r/LocalLLaMA: workstation (9965WX, 512 GB DDR5, 2× RTX 6000 PRO, RAID0 NVMe 29 GB/s), llama.cpp CPU-MoE offload: 0.23 tok/s, 31 min for 400 tokens. (Read through a mirror; Reddit could not be fetched.) ([u/Aroochacha, 2026-07-28](https://www.reddit.com/r/LocalLLaMA/comments/1v9cwfz/i_got_kimik3_running/))
- **K3.1 Pro.** K3.1 keeps falling back to its default persona and largely ignores the user's system prompt; the same prompt worked on DSV4.1 and GLM-5.3. No reply from Moonshot. ([sumiremelody, HF #5 (date approx.), 2026-11-04](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro/discussions/5))
- **K3.1 Pro.** Licence: Model-as-a-Service companies over $20M revenue need a separate agreement; one user's attempt to get one for their company failed. Another says calling it open-weight is misleading, closer to source-available. ([codethief, 2026-11-04](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50369414); [codethief, 2026-11-05](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50380741); [singpolyma3, 2026-11-05](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50374476))

**Positive**

- **K3.** After daily use, K3 and K3.1 feel clearly stronger than DS4.1-Flash-1017, which benchmarks miss; DS Flash is excellent for its price but much smaller. K3 has long topped Design Arena (human preference) among open-weight models. ([reissbaker, 2026-11-04](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50368806))
- **K3.1 Pro.** Switched from Anthropic's API to Kimi K3 (now 3.1 Pro) as a daily driver; goes back to Anthropic about once a month for large refactors, as Anthropic keeps only a small, negligible edge. ([rglover, 2026-11-09](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50426397))
- **K3.1 Pro.** Simon Willison: Artificial Analysis scores K3.1 Pro at 55, behind only Opus 5.5 (58); the max-reasoning pelican drawing is quite solid. API price $2/$10 per M tokens. ([Simon Willison, 2026-10-23](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/23/kimi-k31-pro/))
- **K3.** Unsloth's 2-bit UD-Q2_K_XL holds up well as a daily driver (8×B200), 17 t/s with llama.cpp; 1-bit at 36 tok/s on 4×B200 (Unsloth's pinned post). A commenter warns one generation proves nothing about quant quality. ([anuppillai, unsloth HF #12, 2026-07-31](https://huggingface.co/unsloth/Kimi-K3-GGUF/discussions/12))

**Neutral**

- **K3.1 Pro.** Competitive with Opus 5.5 and a little behind DS V4.1F. ([eunos, 2026-11-05](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50376978))
- **K3.1 Pro.** On the $40 plan, hit the 5-hour limit quickly; output solid enough. Tier list: 1 Opus 5.5, 2 Astra, 3 Argon, 4 Kimi K3.1 Pro. A same-day reply (egeozcan) says Argon is unreliable on big tasks and this undervalues Kimi. ([sail0rm00n, 2026-10-24](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50218402))
- **K3.** Placed between Sonnet/Sol and Astra/Opus, so not at the top level. Its advantage: a company can run it on its own B300s. ([razemio, 2026-11-09](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50423919))
- **K3.1 Pro.** Runs locally on a **MacBook Pro M5 Max with 128 GB** at about **2 tok/s decode, 8.4 tok/s prefill** (argodrive fork, SSD streaming). Same author on HF: one 96 GB GPU, 2 tok/s decode, 40 tok/s prefill. ([Argonautlabs, 2026-11-09](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50431728); [konstantinnikol, HF #12 (date approx.), 2026-11-06](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro/discussions/12))
- **K3.** K3 (4-bit experts, 1.44 TB) on a 128 GB M5 Max, streaming experts from 4 SSDs (internal + 3 TB5 enclosures): 0.55 tok/s with 1 drive, about 1.0 with 4. A reply calls it painful; the author uses it only for overnight batches. Another user: 0.25–0.33 tok/s with WARP on 64/128 GB machines. ([konstantinnikol, HF #185, 2026-09-17](https://huggingface.co/moonshotai/Kimi-K3/discussions/185))
- **K3.1 Pro.** K3.1 had dual-use cyber data removed from pretraining; one user finds it weakened for cyber tasks; a reply says it still beats K3 on cyber because it codes better, and the unrestricted checkpoint was never released. ([mapontosevenths / reissbaker, 2026-11-01](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50321513))

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Fast everyday model, Haiku level, good vision; weak for agentic coding. No comment on French or German. Users wait for a Qwen3.8 version of the 35B-A3B ([ch_sm, 04.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50370244)).

**Negative**

- Not really useful as a coding model; Qwen3.8 27B is the minimum for that ([ekidd, 03.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49944552)).
- Tool mistakes in agents: edits files in the wrong folders ([tripzilch, 23.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49815895)); failed often enough in OpenClaw to be unusable ([sweetjuly, 21.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50188449)).
- Less precise than Gemma 4 26B for structured extraction: more creative and verbose, less exact ([zkmon, 30.04.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47962051)).
- Ollama: broken tool calls give HTTP 500, bug open ([ollama#16383, 01.06.2026](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/16383)).

**Positive**

- Both much faster and much more capable than Haiku, used every day ([sznio, 24.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49837523)).
- About 90 tok/s on an M3 Max (Q6, MLX) ([seanmcdirmid, 14.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49690588)).
- Everyday writing: e-mails, messages, tickets ([LeBit, 24.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49836240)); good for coding and translations ([embedding-shape, 20.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48206909)).
- Vision much better than gemma4-26b-a4b ([jwr, 18.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48182721)); OCR of faded and handwritten receipts ([nullc, 29.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48717401)).

Strata 0.2.13, first released 24.09.2026; not independently verified.) | [github.com/Niko1221/Strata](https://github.com/Niko1221/Strata), [github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/docs/…](https://github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/docs/MAC.md), [huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next…](https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF), [huggingface.co/mlx-community/Qwen3.8-Flash…](https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-4bit) | 2026-11-15 |

Uploaders: ISTA-DASLab (IST Austria lab; GSQ-RCO quantisation, repo created 07.09.2026, IQ3_S added 26.09.2026, about 7.0 M downloads) and unsloth (repo created 26.08.2026, Dynamic v3.1 quants of 04.11.2026, about 2.6 M downloads); both name Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next as base model. Parameter count in the GGUF metadata: 176.94 B for both, against 180.00 B in the official safetensors; the difference matches the 31 `mtp.*` tensors (multi-token prediction head), which these GGUFs do not include (Strata fetches them from the official checkpoint by HTTP range requests, checked against SHA-256). Files: GGUF, Markdown and text only (the unsloth repository also holds an `engram/set_split.py` helper). Licence: unsloth declares the Qwen Community License; ISTA-DASLab's card declares Apache 2.0, which does not match the original. Not done: comparing GGUF tensor headers, and the output check against the publisher's examples (step 7).) | [huggingface.co/api/models/ISTA-DASLab/Qwen…](https://huggingface.co/api/models/ISTA-DASLab/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO-GGUF), [huggingface.co/api/models/unsloth/Qwen3.8-…](https://huggingface.co/api/models/unsloth/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GGUF), [huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next/blo…](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next/blob/main/model.safetensors.index.json), [github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/tools…](https://github.com/Niko1221/Strata/blob/main/tools/mtp_fetch.py) | 2026-11-15 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): Praised on Hacker News; licence not Apache 2.0 or MIT.

**Negative**

- Strata looks promising for weight streaming, but inference crashed before finishing a full answer ([Balinares, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50149101)).

**Positive**

- Used for more than a week with very good results: very fast, very long context windows, on an M5 Max 128 GB ([ttoinou, 02.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49939328)).
- Settled on Qwen3.8 27B/Flash Next on a new M5 Max 128 GB Mac Studio ([fzxu22, 05.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50385925)).
- Qwen3.8-Flash-Next with Strata at about 70 tok/s on an RTX 5070 Ti with 64 GB of DDR4, Q3 quantisation; hard to tell apart from hosted models for coding ([mortalapeman, 06.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50400036)).

- XS 2.1: judged excellent in 6-bit ([mark_l_watson, 10.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50036715)); fast and almost as good for routine coding ([mark_l_watson, 06.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49982882)); used for code reviews on oMLX ([mark_l_watson, 27.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49867113)).
- XS 2.1: 102 tok/s on an M4 Max ([sawfwair, 30.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49112047)).

**Negative**

- Questions recommending qwen2.5-coder-7b (09.2024) as a top coding model in 2026. ([bhelkey, 2026-08-28](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49482849))
- The 1.5B runs fine on old hardware with llama.cpp but makes many mistakes; the user stopped using it. ([testerius, 2026-10-13](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50072570))

**Positive**

- A 7B Qwen2.5 Coder is useful locally for autocomplete while programming. ([cogman10, 2026-09-04](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49564136))

**Neutral**

- In Zed, edit predictions did nothing with `prompt_format: infer`; setting `qwen` explicitly fixes it. ([zed #59092, 2026-06-16](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/issues/59092#issuecomment-4716292414))
- Sweep's founder: Qwen3 scores worse than Qwen2.5 for autocomplete because it was never pretrained for FIM (vendor). ([kevinlu1248, 2026-01-23](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46727316))

**Negative**

- **Licence**: the card says Apache 2.0, but the LICENSE file is missing (404), also in the GGUF repository ([HF #52, 18.07.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next/discussions/52); [HF #29, 11.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next/discussions/29)).

- No thinking mode, so it has to go over its work several times ([Tade0, 03.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50350667)); ignores simple instructions ([HF #50, 18.06.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-Next/discussions/50)).
- Ollama's tool-call parser rejects some long file writes ([ollama#18563, open](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/18563)).

**Positive**

- 60–80 tok/s on an M4 Max ([ttoinou, 21.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47096491); [LoganDark, 12.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48508826)).
- The best results so far for one user ([Shorel, 13.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48520417)).

| runtimes | Ollama official qwen3.8:27b (MLX build memory blow-up on M5 Max 128 GB, #17875 closed, fix unconfirmed; HTTP 500 in long tool loops, #17778 open); llama.cpp llama-server with GGUF (ggml-org, unsloth, bartowski); LM Studio (lmstudio-community GGUF and MLX 4/5/6/8-bit); mlx-lm with mlx-community 4bit (Ollama issue status from an earlier check (13.11.2026), not re-checked. Vision path (mmproj) on llama.cpp/MLX not verified for this model.) | [ollama.com/library/qwen3.8](https://ollama.com/library/qwen3.8), [huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen3.8-27B-GGUF](https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/Qwen3.8-27B-GGUF), [huggingface.co/lmstudio-community/Qwen3.8-…](https://huggingface.co/lmstudio-community/Qwen3.8-27B-MLX-8bit), [huggingface.co/mlx-community/Qwen3.8-27B-4bit](https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/Qwen3.8-27B-4bit), [github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/17875](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/17875) | 2026-11-14 |
| fits_128gb | yes | see size_gb | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Seen as the best local coder of its size; lower the thinking effort; watch Ollama MLX memory on the M5 Max. Rumours of Qwen4 (HN, 09.11.2026), not a release.

**Negative**

- Thinks far too much by default (xhigh): one task took 11 hours, but gave the best result of any self-hostable model ([SwellJoe, 17.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49325586)). Advice: set the effort to medium or low ([c7b, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49304853)).
- Thinking loops, no code written after 30 min with a Q3 quant ([cdurth, 26.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49457293)); tool-call loops with thinking off ([HF #243, 11.11.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B/discussions/243)).
- Dense, so 7–8× slower than the 35B-A3B ([andy99, 16.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49325030)).
- **On an M5 Max with 128 GB**: Ollama's MLX build used all the memory until the system crashed ([ollama#17875, 24.08.2026](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/17875); closed, fix not confirmed). HTTP 500 in long tool loops ([ollama#17778, open](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/17778)).

**Positive**

- Fixes bugs Qwen 3.6 could not, without ever getting stuck in a loop ([madduci, 17.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49326625)); near Opus 4.6 in one user's benchmark ([theshrike79, 17.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49327464)).
- Daily driver with opencode ([zkmon, 05.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50378227)); a decent replacement for Sonnet, though slower ([sznio, 24.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49837523)).
- 59.5 tok/s on an M4 Max 128 GB ([asats, 23.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49405633)).
- French: seems to know French noticeably better than Copilot ([dexterlagan, 15.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49308753)). German: a small improvement, with some regressions ([scirob, 15.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49310104)).
- Also reads images ([ALLTaken, 20.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49380951)).

**Negative**

- In Zed with llama.cpp, local Sweep 1.5B keeps proposing newlines at random places (open bug). ([zed #62635, 2026-08-14](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/issues/62635))

- Neovim plugin author: super impressed by performance and quality, integrated it into cursortab.nvim. ([leonardcser, 2026-01-22](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46717434))
- Added it as a Zed edit-prediction provider on llama.cpp on a MacBook Pro; works well, including next edit. ([ihales, 2026-01-23](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46734093))
- The Zed PR adding self-hosted Sweep says personal testing shows impressive quality. ([zed PR #51139, 2026-07-30](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/pull/51139))

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Zeta 2 has about 30 % higher acceptance than Zeta 1 (Zed). Self-hosting needed fixes (prompt format, max_output_tokens 256). Conservative suggestions. More useful since Zed removed edit predictions from its free plan (07.10.2026).

**Negative**

- Self-hosted Zeta 2 proposed deleting most of the file: Zed sent Qwen-style FIM tokens while Zeta 2 expects Seed-Coder tokens in SPM order. ([zed #52585, 2026-03-31](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/issues/52585#issuecomment-4162121354))

**Positive**

- Recommends zeta-2 (bartowski GGUF) for autocomplete instead of a general Qwen3.6 model. ([recov, 2026-04-16](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47794567))
- After Zed's fix, self-hosted Zeta 2 worked once max_output_tokens was raised to 256. ([zed #52585, 2026-05-11](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/issues/52585#issuecomment-4420727863))
- With Ollama and `prompt_format: zeta2`, the local model gave the same experience as Zed's hosted predictions. ([GolmEi, 2026-05-10](https://huggingface.co/zed-industries/zeta-2/discussions/1))

**Neutral**

- Zeta2 is very conservative and rarely proposes more than a few obvious lines; mostly saves typing. ([ekidd, 2026-07-30](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49109764))
- After Zed moved edit predictions out of the free plan: Zeta 2.1 is an 8B that fits most computers at Q4. ([rarisma, 2026-08-28](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49483373))

**Negative**

- MLX batch inference leaked about 50 GB on Apple Silicon with 50–100 objects, fixed by PR #3 on 07.04.2026 ([#2, 02.04.2026](https://github.com/tiiuae/Falcon-Perception/issues/2)); the server tried to allocate 56 GB on 24 GB GPUs, KV-cache size had to be hard-coded ([#5, 05.04.2026](https://github.com/tiiuae/Falcon-Perception/issues/5)).
- Prompt-based detection works "only in some cases" for small, occluded or reflective objects ([#25, 11.06.2026](https://github.com/tiiuae/Falcon-Perception/issues/25)).

**Neutral**

- The Hugging Face main branch is not synced with a GitHub patch ([HF discussion #4, 12.05.2026](https://huggingface.co/tiiuae/Falcon-Perception/discussions/4)).

For OCR, Google recommends an image budget of 1120 tokens.) | [ollama.com/library/gemma4](https://ollama.com/library/gemma4), [github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/21631](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/21631), [github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/16562](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/16562), [github.com/lmstudio-ai/lmstudio-bug-tracke…](https://github.com/lmstudio-ai/lmstudio-bug-tracker/issues/2265) | 2026-11-15 |
| fits_128gb | yes (every size fits, bf16 included (31B bf16 62.6 GB)) | see size_gb | 2026-11-15 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback rich): Users praise the 12B and the 26B-A4B for image understanding, handwriting and speed; the 26B-A4B is very fast on Apple silicon. Captions are less specific than Qwen3.8-27B or MiniCPM-V 4.7 in one sweep. Weak points: tool calling and instruction following (31B), template loops (fixed in July), runtime bugs in Ollama and LM Studio on Macs. No report on French, German or Italian OCR.

**Negative**

- Qwen 3.5/3.6 35B-A3B "WAY better with vision" than the 26B-A4B ([jwr, 18.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48182721)); a reply asks whether the image budget was the default 280 tokens ([argee, 19.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48195509)).
- 31B "absurdly weak" at tool calling and instruction following ([coder543, 23.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48648489)).
- Coin classification: 53 percent correct ([dgacmu, 02.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49933256)).
- 12B: about 1 in 3,000 adult video frames mis-captioned as involving a child, a risk for moderation ([philipkglass, 21.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48994615)).
- 12B: degenerate `thought` loops on long agent prompts; chat-template fix by Google on 14.07.2026 ([HF 12B #41](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-4-12B-it/discussions/41)).
- LM Studio does not expose Gemma's image budget, which "made gemma4 useless" for one user ([jwr, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49301283)).

**Positive**

- 12B: the most effective vision model one user tested, including models several times its size ([SwellJoe, 23.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48645351)).
- 12B 4-bit used to sort, classify and describe images ([SwellJoe, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49304989)).
- 12B reads tiny text, gives bounding boxes and transcribes audio, 7 GB with its projector ([CMay, 17.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49327574)).
- 12B QAT image analysis called the most interesting open release of the year ([dofm, 15.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50104808)).
- 26B-A4B: best handwriting recognition one user tried, ahead of Qwen3-VL-8B and GLM-OCR ([physicles, 22.04.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47870197)).
- 26B-A4B: solid image analysis and good OCR on very tough images ([ekidd, 22.04.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47860412)).
- 26B-A4B via llama.cpp for privacy-sensitive image processing ([deniska, 09.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50023492)).
- 26B-A4B beats Mistral Small 4 on a business benchmark that includes German writing ([nik736, 08.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49611637)).
- 31B: French→English round trip "perfectly readable", a few awkward phrasings ([lambda, 13.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48511050)).
- QAT 4-bit "indistinguishable from the full-fat version" ([SwellJoe, 13.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49281788)).

**Neutral**

- 26B-A4B and 12B: clean captions but less specific than Qwen3.8-27B and MiniCPM-V 4.7 in a captioning sweep ([mlx-vlm #2546, 17.10.2026](https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-vlm/issues/2546)).

| provenance | consistent: 370.3 M fp32 parameters; 1,481,204,170 bytes against 1,481,333,394 for the official model.pt (pickle overhead); converted with mlx-vlm (PR #2171, merged by a maintainer); fp32 check against PyTorch reported by the port author: identical masks, under 0.2 % median depth error (uploaded 02.09.2026 by an mlx-community member; no base_model in the card; 140 downloads, no discussion) | [huggingface.co/mlx-community/moge-3-vitl-m…](https://huggingface.co/mlx-community/moge-3-vitl-mlx-fp32) | 2026-11-15 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Edge refinement can amplify depth errors; an independent reproduction falls far short of the paper on KITTI (outdoor), better on indoor sets; one user prefers MoGe-2 normals.

**Negative**

- "Quality is not as good as described"; the refinement "improves some edges, but it also amplifies depth errors" ([MoGe #171, 13–16.10.2026](https://github.com/microsoft/MoGe/issues/171)). Independent reproduction of ViT-L far below the paper on KITTI (δ1 55.3 against 85.1), better on NYUv2 and ETH3D; maintainers investigating ([#173, 21.10.2026](https://github.com/microsoft/MoGe/issues/173)).
- Normals worse than `moge-2-vitl-normal` for one user ([#163, 03.09.2026](https://github.com/microsoft/MoGe/issues/163)).

**Neutral**

- "Models like MapAnything/MoGe predict calibration params directly… We still calibrate manually on-site, but mainly to validate these models actually hold up." ([HN, 09.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48465090))
- MLX port: the reviewer says "lgtm"; under 0.2 % median depth difference against PyTorch, self-reported by the port author ([mlx-vlm PR #2171, 05.09.2026](https://github.com/Blaizzy/mlx-vlm/pull/2171)).

**Negative**

- Slow: 15–30 s per image; quality quite good, if a bit verbose ([vunderba, 13.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48117409)).
- Ollama cannot send it video ([ollama#18151](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/18151)); the thinking variant can loop without answering ([ollama#17978](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/17978)).

**Positive**

- Used daily to caption NAS photos into EXIF and a search index ([vunderba, 14.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48141957)).
- For its possible replacement: the vision of Qwen3.6 is judged excellent ([scotty79, 15.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48544119)).

**Positive**

- Still the usual choice for local search and RAG; 32K context ([donhardman, 17.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49739903)). No newer Qwen text-embedding family.

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Widely used on Macs, including the 0.6B in local agent pipelines. Vendor scores: 4B MMTEB-R 72.7, 8B 72.9, bge-reranker-v2-m3 58.4; MTEB maintainers could not reproduce Qwen's reranking scores. Most community GGUFs lack the classifier head and return scores near 0. llama.cpp decoder rerank is about 6x slower than necessary (open issue).

**Negative**

- MTEB maintainers could not reproduce Qwen's reported reranking scores for 0.6B/4B/8B; implementation merged anyway. ([AyushM6, 2026-06-22](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-4B/discussions/12))
- llama.cpp /v1/rerank returned ~1e-20 scores because the community GGUF lacked the classifier head; the Voodisss 4B GGUF works. ([llama.cpp #25447, 2026-07-08](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/25447))
- With --reranking on decoder models, llama.cpp copies full-vocab logits for every token, making the 0.6B about 6x slower; still open. ([llama.cpp #30422, 2026-10-12](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/30422))
- Users still wait for native reranking in Ollama; the feature request remains open. ([ollama #3368, 2026-07-23](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/3368#issuecomment-5055347102))

**Positive**

- Show HN author runs a whole agent pipeline locally on a MacBook with Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B. ([ymattia, 2026-10-16](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50120300))
- A team chose Qwen3-Reranker 0.6B and 4B as the base for a LoRA fine-tune scoring sigmoid(logit_yes - logit_no). ([trashhalo, 2026-06-11](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48493986))

**Neutral**

- The prompt format must be followed exactly: the reference input closes an empty <think></think> block and scores only the yes/no logits. ([CyberNativeAI, 2026-10-03](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-4B/discussions/8))

| runtimes | official Ollama tag, LM Studio, llama.cpp; harmony format required | Ollama, model card | 2026-11-13 |
| fits_128gb | yes | size | 2026-11-13 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): Users like writing a policy (e.g. prompt-injection techniques) and running it on web fetch and search results only. Reasoning makes it take seconds. Chat-template bug with tool calls.

**Negative**

- The chat template raises a TypeError on an assistant message with content null and tool_calls, the normal shape in agent loops; fix proposed. ([HF discussion #8, 2026-07-27](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-safeguard-20b/discussions/8))

**Positive**

- Dedicated prompt-injection models were limited; gets good results from gpt-oss-safeguard with a policy describing injection techniques, run on web fetch and search results. ([HN comment, 2026-10-19](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50147437))
- Uses gpt-oss-safeguard-20b for moderation tasks, via a hosted API. ([HN comment, 2026-09-23](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49819683))

| Fact | Value | Source | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|

| gated | no | Hugging Face API | 2026-11-13 |
| publisher | Qwen (Alibaba) | Hugging Face API | 2026-11-13 |

| parameters_total_b | 8B: 8.2; 4B: 4.4; 0.6B: 0.75 | Hugging Face API, safetensors totals | 2026-11-14 |

| fits_128gb | yes | sizes | 2026-11-13 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): 9 categories plus refusal detection, Safe/Controversial/Unsafe, no custom policies. Highest recall in one independent 2026 benchmark of 14 guards (arXiv 2605.28830). Sizes below 8B missed a plain prompt-injection request (ignore previous instructions).

**Negative**

- Every size below 8B classified a classic prompt-injection request (ignore previous instructions, reveal the system prompt) as Safe. ([HF discussion #14, 2026-05-17](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3Guard-Gen-8B/discussions/14))
- A secondary-semantic attack (a harmless poster prompt whose characters spell an attack phrase) passed: input Controversial, generated HTML Safe. ([QwenLM/Qwen3Guard #24, 2026-06-01](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3Guard/issues/24))
- Stream variant: reusing a stream id makes latency grow until it hangs (CUDA and MPS), and labels drift from Unsafe to Safe; remote code incompatible with transformers 5.x. ([QwenLM/Qwen3Guard #29, 2026-09-22](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3Guard/issues/29))

**Positive**

- Stream variant: a community PR adds KV caching, 0.6B on an Apple M4 (MPS) from 116 to 28.7 ms per token; merged. ([HF PR #4 (Stream-8B), 2026-09-19](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3Guard-Stream-8B/discussions/4))

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Liked for speed and tables; Ollama is the weak point (loops, context); FR/DE untested.

**Negative**

- On Ollama, output loops forever, still open ([ollama#16892, 24.06.2026](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/16892)); Ollama 0.35.1 broke table recognition ([ollama#18810, 05.10.2026](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/18810)).
- Ollama's default context (4096) is too small: empty output or crash; set `num_ctx` to about 10240 ([HF #8, 06.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-OCR/discussions/8); [ollama#16696, 12.06.2026](https://github.com/ollama/ollama/issues/16696)).
- Hallucinations, schema not followed ([unfocso, 16.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48937089)).
- Z.ai says languages other than Chinese and English are not yet fully optimised: a warning for French and German accents ([HF #41, 25.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/zai-org/GLM-OCR/discussions/41)).

**Positive**

- Does much better than Claude, on modest hardware ([llm_nerd, 05.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49577756)).
- Slightly less accurate than Datalab Chandra, but faster; PaddleOCR clearly worse ([phenomen, 24.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49421088)).
- Good on handwriting ([vinaigrette, 01.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48750605)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback none): Small, Apache 2.0, a single safetensors file (no `.py`, unlike PaddleOCR-VL). Almost no feedback: read the Hugging Face discussions and test before deciding.

**Positive**

- Good experiences with MinerU and PaddleOCR-VL ([dcan, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49298968)); version not stated.

- Used on Mac as an MLX 8-bit build ([dandaka, 21.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49387167)); good experiences with MinerU and PaddleOCR-VL ([dcan, 14.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49298968)).

**Negative**

- On 2,000 test images, some returned no layout result at all, especially small images, including 52 English pages; translated from Chinese ([HF #6, 10.03.2026, launch](https://huggingface.co/PaddlePaddle/PP-DocLayoutV3/discussions/6)).

**Positive**

- Safetensors (PyTorch) is the fastest of the three back-ends, about 42 ms per image against 55 ms for ONNX and 64 ms for Paddle, on an RTX 5060 Ti ([HF #8, 21.03.2026, launch](https://huggingface.co/PaddlePaddle/PP-DocLayoutV3/discussions/8)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Version 1 praised on Hacker News as very good and cheap to run locally, but one side-by-side test on a hard scan found it the fastest and the least faithful: it invented text. Almost no reports on version 2 yet.

**Negative**

- Version 1, same hard low-resolution scan through five models: fastest but least faithful, it wrote plausible text that is not in the document; faithfulness order olmOCR-2, Qianfan-OCR, PaddleOCR-VL, HunyuanOCR, Unlimited-OCR. Tall pages need the cropping mode, otherwise it repeats itself ([rhirae, HF #3, 25.06.2026](https://huggingface.co/baidu/Unlimited-OCR/discussions/3)).
- Version 1 breaks with recent vLLM and Transformers 5 after a config change; workaround by pinning an older revision ([HF #23, 16–30.10.2026](https://huggingface.co/baidu/Unlimited-OCR/discussions/23)).

**Positive**

- Version 1 judged very good and cheap to run on a decent computer ([HDBaseT, 30.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48740641); [Johnny_Bonk, 13.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49289852)); fits in 12 GB of VRAM and seems competitive ([potsandpans, 04.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48786469)).

**Neutral**

- Version 2 runs on Apple Silicon through the mlx-community build ([HF #2, 28.10.2026](https://huggingface.co/baidu/Unlimited-OCR-2/discussions/2)); users ask for Turkish and Arabic, more languages promised ([HF #1, 27.10.2026](https://huggingface.co/baidu/Unlimited-OCR-2/discussions/1)).

| runtimes | mlx-audio (mlx-community/Qwen3-ASR-1.7B-8bit; server truncation of long audio fixed in #1008); llama.cpp mtmd (ggml-org/Qwen3-ASR-1.7B-GGUF, Metal); transformers native since 26.06.2026 but via the separate Qwen/Qwen3-ASR-1.7B-hf repo; official qwen-asr package, transformers backend on MPS (sdpa: clips > 8 s get full attention, issue #213 open). vLLM backend is CUDA-oriented (Issue #213 does not name MPS explicitly, but sdpa is what MPS uses because flash-attn is CUDA-only, so it applies on a Mac. qwen-asr on MPS and llama.cpp on Metal are not backed by any Mac user report found. vLLM on Mac (vllm-metal) does not list Qwen3-ASR.) | [github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-ASR](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-ASR) (README, issue #213); https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio (README, #1008); https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/multimodal.md; transformers docs model_doc/qwen3_asr | 2026-11-14 |
| fits_128gb | yes | see size_gb | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Strong Whisper replacement on MLX; split long files into chunks; check French punctuation.

**Negative**

- **French**: punctuation lost on long audio, giving large blocks of unstructured text ([QwenLM/Qwen3-ASR#100, 25.02.2026](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-ASR/issues/100)).
- Repetition loops ([HF #23, 27.04.2026, older](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-ASR-1.7B/discussions/23)).
- mlx-audio server truncated long audio (`max_tokens=1024`), now fixed ([mlx-audio#1008, 09.10.2026](https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio/issues/1008)).

**Positive**

- With MLX on macOS, copes much better with background noise than Whisper or Parakeet ([sidclaw, 29.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49100131)).
- Satisfied with transcription quality and speed; better than Parakeet ([skolos, 08.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50013705)); better than Voxtral ([jiehong, 28.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50280418)).

Yes.') on some long recordings, seen with MLX 4-bit and with bf16 transformers (VibeVoice#373 open) (Official demo and transformers on MPS: code path exists, no Mac user report found. A GGUF/C++ runtime for llama.cpp is an open PR (#30530), not merged.) | [github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio](https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio) (README); https://github.com/microsoft/VibeVoice (demo/vibevoice_asr_inference_from_file.py, docs/vibevoice-asr.md, issue #373); https://huggingface.co/microsoft/VibeVoice-ASR/discussions/10 | 2026-11-14 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Unique one-pass speaker separation; unfixed loops; multilingual quality doubtful.

**Negative**

- Generation loops (the same word repeated) or never stops, still in October ([HF #22, 14.03 and 07.10.2026](https://huggingface.co/microsoft/VibeVoice-ASR/discussions/22)).
- Hallucinates a lot, very heavy and slow, poor in multilingual use ([steinvakt2, 28.04.2026, older](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47933955)).
- Overlapping speech poorly handled ([HF #5, 22.01.2026](https://huggingface.co/microsoft/VibeVoice-ASR/discussions/5)).

**Positive**

- MLX 4-bit on a 16 GB M2: faster than real time, almost the same as bf16 ([HF #10, 08.10.2026](https://huggingface.co/microsoft/VibeVoice-ASR/discussions/10)).
- More reliable than Whisper or Parakeet; built-in speaker separation seen as a big advantage ([mberg, 28.04.2026, older](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47935982)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Good French and English streaming; language detection fragile, a risk for German; use an MLX runtime.

**Negative**

- No language hint: **German** first transcribed as Portuguese; Italian mistaken for other languages ([HF #19, 12–16.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/mistralai/Voxtral-Mini-4B-Realtime-2602/discussions/19)).
- When the speaker switches language, it may translate instead of transcribing (French ↔ Spanish) ([HF #21, 13.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/mistralai/Voxtral-Mini-4B-Realtime-2602/discussions/21)).
- Some community ports are slow on Mac ([refulgentis, 10.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46955514)).
- Voxtral 3 is API-only: this is the last open version ([T0mSIlver, 21.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50178093)).

**Positive**

- Over 95 % success in English, **French** and Spanish ([HF #21, 14.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/mistralai/Voxtral-Mini-4B-Realtime-2602/discussions/21)); impressive results on poor-quality audio in French and English ([blobinabottle, 04.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46890192)).
- macOS dictation app via MLX: a dramatic improvement over Whisper ([T0mSIlver, 25.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47149479)).

- Quality judged excellent on Mac and PC ([armcat, 21.08.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49392664)); still used as a specialty model ([itsdavesanders, 05.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50379352)).

**Negative**

- Not a usable release: cloning promised in February, still missing in May; voices speed up past about 15 s and stop near 40 s ([HF #8, 06.02–30.05.2026](https://huggingface.co/kugelaudio/kugelaudio-0-open/discussions/8)).

**Positive**

- Works great in French, cloning very good with the Space code; long texts accelerate, generate sentence by sentence ([kugelaudio-open #1, 16.01.2026](https://github.com/Kugelaudio/kugelaudio-open/issues/1)).

Base cloning degenerates when ref_text does not cover the whole reference clip (mlx-audio#921, reproduced with the official PyTorch code too) (vllm-metal documents only the 0.6B CustomVoice; the 1.7B CustomVoice and Base there are unverified. flash-attn, recommended in the README, is CUDA-only.) | [github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio](https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio) (README, issue #921); https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS (README, issue #69); https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm-metal/blob/main/docs/omni.md | 2026-11-14 |

**Negative**

- Cross-language cloning keeps the original accent; the speaker sometimes changes ([QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS#134, 29.01.2026](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS/issues/134); [#80, 14.02.2026](https://github.com/QwenLM/Qwen3-TTS/issues/80)).
- Slow, not practical for real time ([rhdunn, 23.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49820470)).
- mlx-audio: Base cloning produces garbage when `ref_text` does not match the whole reference clip ([mlx-audio#921, 28.08.2026](https://github.com/Neywa-Labs/mlx-audio/issues/921)).
- One team switched to Chatterbox, found more predictable and reliable ([HF CustomVoice #28, 20.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-TTS-12Hz-1.7B-CustomVoice/discussions/28)).

**Positive**

- Used almost daily for voice cloning with mlx-audio ([rahimnathwani, 21.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48986493)); another user is fully convinced by its voice cloning ([jsemrau, 07.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48825291)).
- **German**: worked well in a German test ([MacNCheese23, 23.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49821085)). Nothing on French.

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Quality called very good; in one small independent German test, fewer word errors than Qwen3-TTS and Chatterbox Multilingual; cloned speech sometimes too fast; early MPS noise on M5 fixed.

**Negative**

- Quality called incredibly good, but cloned speech sometimes comes out two to three times too fast; changing the seed helped ([HF #3, 08.04–14.09.2026](https://huggingface.co/openbmb/VoxCPM2/discussions/3)).

**Positive**

- Third-party benchmark on 10 FLEURS pairs per language: German word error rate 3.8 % against 10.8 % for Qwen3-TTS Base and 5.2 % for Chatterbox Multilingual; speaker similarity 0.73 against 0.78 for Chatterbox ([HF #17, 02.07.2026](https://huggingface.co/openbmb/VoxCPM2/discussions/17), [soniqo.audio](https://www.soniqo.audio/blog/voice-cloning-benchmarks)).

**Neutral**

- Cross-lingual cloning keeps an accent; naming the target language in the control instruction largely removes it ([HF #14, 14.05.2026](https://huggingface.co/openbmb/VoxCPM2/discussions/14)).
- Noise on an M5 Mac with MPS in bf16, fixed in a later version ([VoxCPM #301, 10–19.05.2026](https://github.com/OpenBMB/VoxCPM/issues/301)); a LoRA voice adapter trained on it for an assistant ([HN, 09.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49623246)).

**Negative**

- Skips the curly apostrophe (’), e.g. "nurse’s" read as "nurse"; Zyphra confirms and promises a text normaliser ([HF #4, 13.11.2026](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/discussions/4)).

**Neutral**

- Urdu cloning good but pronunciation inconsistent, 15.3 % word error rate measured by Zyphra ([HF #3, 12.11.2026](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/discussions/3)); any language works with any voice ([HF #1, 11.11.2026](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/discussions/1)).

**Negative**

- Lucida almost always leaves smears and leftovers in ComfyUI; the v13 fix regressed and was reverted to v7. ([HF lucida #1, 2026-07-20](https://huggingface.co/egeorcun/lucida/discussions/1))

**Positive**

- Recommends rembg for local background removal, with the birefnet model for photos and toonout for illustrations. ([vunderba, 2026-08-24](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49427056))

**Neutral**

- The author confirms the Hugging Face weights may be used freely under the MIT licence, while noting uncertainty about the training data. ([HF discussion #23, 2026-11-04](https://huggingface.co/ZhengPeng7/BiRefNet/discussions/23))
- At SAM 3's launch, BiRefNet v2 was still slightly better for cutting out children's drawings. ([Benjamin_Dobell, 2025-11-19](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=45986203))

- Judged very poor: not better than its contemporaries in any respect ([SV_BubbleTime, 20.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49781048)).
- Outputs can look artificial ([vunderba, 20.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49776784)).

**Positive**

- Edit variant: judged very good, clearly better than Flux.2 Klein 9B and Qwen-Edit ([woadwarrior01, 22.06.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48634277)).
- Scored 6/15 on the GenAI Showdown text-to-image benchmark, against 4/15 for Qwen-Image 1.0/2512, with good coherence ([vunderba, 20.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49776784)); later called underrated and quite decent ([vunderba, 17.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50127184)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): Fastest image model on an M5 Max, good for editing; weak anatomy and spelling in text-to-image.

**Negative**

- The Klein family is mainly for editing; in text-to-image it brings back the distorted bodies of the SDXL era ([vunderba, 24.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49038190), about the 9B).
- The 4B needs retries when exact spelling matters (blog test, lead only: [wiro.ai](https://wiro.ai/blog/flux-2-klein-base-4b-vs-9b-5-prompt-image-test/)).

**Positive**

- **M5 Max 128 GB with mflux**: about 0.8–1.0 s per step at q4, about 4–5 s per image ([fxd0h, 03.10.2026](https://github.com/mflux-community/mflux/issues/817)).
- Practically the best option on most consumer hardware ([Tenoke, 24.07.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49032727)).
- Successor FLUX.3 Klein (gated, licence "other") judged very disappointing compared with Flux 2 Klein ([Thorentis, 16.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50125486)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback none): No user reports. Diffusion edit: regenerates the whole image, so faces can drift; recombine its colours with the original lightness (Lab).

**Positive**

- Uses AI to colourise old black-and-white pictures for a history blog (category). ([strenholme, 2026-09-06](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49586100))

**Neutral**

- The author explains the difference with FlatLog2TrueColor: log footage is flat and desaturated, B&W has no colour, so the two LoRAs target different inputs. ([makisekurisu-jp / prithivMLmods, 2026-03-09](https://huggingface.co/prithivMLmods/QIE-2511-FlatLog2TrueColor/discussions/2))

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback thin): Good prompt understanding, last Apache 2.0 Qwen-Image; mixed reports on spelling and artifacts; heavy (20B).

**Negative**

- Could not spell correctly; letters were often garbled ([ranger_danger, 10.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46962927)).
- Grid pattern in dark areas ([HF #10, 02.01.2026](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen-Image-2512/discussions/10)); VAE high-frequency artifacts, 40 GB at FP16 ([SV_BubbleTime, 10.02.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46963112)).
- Looks more artificial (more "AI") than Z-Image Turbo ([CamperBob2, 17.01.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46655786)).
- Successor Qwen-Image 2.1 scores better (7/15 against 4/15) but is non-commercial ([vunderba, 20.09.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49780215)).

**Positive**

- With Z-Image and Flux.2, far ahead of SDXL on complex scenes ([vunderba, 20.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48201311)).

**Positive**

- Generate video locally with LTX 2.3 or WAN, then upscale with SeedVR2. ([vunderba, 2026-05-15](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48155014))

**Neutral**

- The latest ComfyUI update broke the numz nodes; the maintainer advises ComfyUI's native SeedVR2 implementation. ([HF numz #6, 2026-11-05](https://huggingface.co/numz/SeedVR2_comfyUI/discussions/6))

- LTX Video 2.3, or even an older WAN, could easily handle such a task ([vunderba, 15.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48155014)).

One cause: the `uni_pc` sampler; `euler` works ([#16573, 26.09.2026](https://github.com/Comfy-Org/ComfyUI/issues/16573)).
- Diffusers text-encoder bug (see above) ([HF #6, 07–11.02.2026](https://huggingface.co/Wan-AI/Wan2.2-TI2V-5B-Diffusers/discussions/6)).

**Positive**

- Still used, but LTX 2.3 is now the usual fast local choice ([vunderba, 15.05.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48155014)).

Exception needed; github `CSSLab/maia3` [info] – AGPL-3.0 code, UCI executables maia3-5m/23m/79m.

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-13, feedback moderate): Maia predicts human moves (Maia-3 57.1 % move match vs 52.0 % for Maia-2, vendor). Players enjoy it as a sparring partner. Maia-2 was not trained on the first 5 plies, so its openings are poor (the website uses an opening book). Recent Maia-3 issues: misses simple tactics, fixed seed, monotonous weak play at high Elo locally, AGPL friction.

**Negative**

- Maia-2 shuffles knights back and forth in the opening; CSSLab: the first 5 plies were excluded from training. ([maia2#5, 2025-07-17](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia2/issues/5); [maia2#13, 2026-01-15](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia2/issues/13))
- Maia-3 fails to predict what most players would play once a simple tactic (mate in 2) is on the board. ([maia3#5, 2026-05-27](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/5))
- Maia-3 79M locally in En Croissant at Elo 2600 gave monotonous, weak play, unlike maiachess.com at the same level. ([maia3#15, 2026-10-04](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/15))
- Maia-3 seed always 42 (identical games after restart), play collapses above Elo 2800, 'cp' scores are not centipawns. ([maia3#17, 2026-11-05](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/17); [maia3#19, 2026-11-05](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/19))
- The AGPL licence makes Maia-3 harder for others to use; several open issues ask CSSLab to clarify the checkpoint licence, unanswered. ([HF Maia3-79M #1, 2026-06-20](https://huggingface.co/UofTCSSLab/Maia3-79M/discussions/1); [maia3#8, 2026-07-15](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/8); [maia3#11, 2026-08-20](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia3/issues/11))

**Positive**

- Launch post by the developer: Maia predicts the exact human move over 50 % of the time; meant as a sparring partner and teaching aid. ([reidmcy (Show HN), 2020-12-01](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=25268658))
- A 1560-rated player found it a real pleasure to play against, with human-like habits such as dropping pieces in complex exchanges. ([sabujp, 2021-01-17](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=25813731))
- Use it to find the mistakes a player at a given level would make and turn them into exercises; the developer says this is one of the goals. ([PeterisP, 2021-01-17](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=25810726); [reidmcy, 2021-01-17](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=25813083))
- Maia-3 launch: an effective drop-in replacement for the Leela Chess Zero model that outperformed the best existing LC0 model. ([free_bip, 2026-05-27](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48298645))
- Human-oriented analysis is better served by Maia's approach (a net trained on human moves) than by tuning Stockfish. ([tsoj, 2026-09-10](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49643564))

**Neutral**

- Maia-2 training could not run outside CUDA on a Mac; fixed in July 2026, the README now lists Apple MPS. ([maia2#8, 2025-09-16](https://github.com/CSSLab/maia2/issues/8))

### Chronos-2 (Amazon)

File: [`models/chronos-2.json`](models/chronos-2.json).

Sources: huggingface `amazon/chronos-2`.

| Fact | Value | Source | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|
| licence | Apache 2.0 (card tag and GitHub repository; no LICENSE file in the weights repository) | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2), [github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting) | 2026-11-15 |
| gated | no | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| publisher | Amazon | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| release_date | 2025-10-30 | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| parameters_total_b | 0.12 | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| size_gb | 0.48 (a 28M variant exists: autogluon/chronos-2-small, 0.11 GB) | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| formats | safetensors; no .py | [huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2](https://huggingface.co/amazon/chronos-2) | 2026-11-15 |
| runtimes | pip `chronos-forecasting` (≥ 2.0; `predict_df` takes a pandas table); CPU; Apple GPU (MPS) undocumented. Pin `transformers<5` unless the fix of PR #416 is confirmed (issue #491) | [github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecast…](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting/issues/491) | 2026-11-15 |
| fits_128gb | yes | see size_gb | 2026-11-15 |

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Most mature and simplest to use; strongest independent live record (57 weeks); open bugs on empty series and a mislabelled output; Transformers 5 broke loading.

**Negative**

- Common to all forecasting foundation models: practitioners report they often do not beat simple baselines on their own data: TimesFM about ARIMA level while bigger and slower ([magimas, 31.03.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47583870)); seasonal naive usually wins for e-commerce ([jc4p, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50155295)); one XGBoost model per series still wins ([niltecedu, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50156050)); XGBoost and LightGBM beat transformers on blood glucose ([articulatepang, 14.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50498722)). The benefit is many series without building one model each ([logancbrown, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50152506)). Backtest against a simple baseline first.
- An entirely empty (NaN) series gives absurd ±1e5 quantiles; one real value avoids it ([#544, 05.11.2026, open](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting/issues/544)); the second output of `predict_quantiles` is documented as the mean but is the median ([#542, 22.10.2026, open](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting/issues/542)).
- Transformers 5 silently broke the loaded weights; maintainer advises `transformers<5` until the fix ([#491, 17.04.2026](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting/issues/491); [#519, 28.07.2026, closed](https://github.com/amazon-science/chronos-forecasting/issues/519)).

**Neutral**

- Benchmarks (leads, not user feedback): TS-Arena 60.3 over 57 weeks [58.6–61.9] on 14.11.2026, a live benchmark where forecasts are submitted before the data exists, mostly electricity data, higher is better ([TS-Arena](https://ts-arena.live)); GIFT-Eval relative CRPS / MASE 0.485 / 0.698, recalculated from the published result files, not the official ranking, lower is better ([results](https://github.com/SalesforceAIResearch/gift-eval/tree/main/results)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Best accuracy among plain Apache models on both benchmarks; called the leader by one commenter; one month old, small company, no DataFrame input yet.

**Negative**

- Common to all forecasting foundation models: practitioners report they often do not beat simple baselines on their own data: TimesFM about ARIMA level while bigger and slower ([magimas, 31.03.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47583870)); seasonal naive usually wins for e-commerce ([jc4p, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50155295)); one XGBoost model per series still wins ([niltecedu, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50156050)); XGBoost and LightGBM beat transformers on blood glucose ([articulatepang, 14.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50498722)). The benefit is many series without building one model each ([logancbrown, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50152506)). Backtest against a simple baseline first.

**Positive**

- Called "the leader", with Timestral close behind ([rubenfiszel, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50152944)); a t0 author says win rates against Timestral are tight and advises trying both, vendor comment ([GeoffNN, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50151727)).

**Neutral**

- Benchmarks (leads, not user feedback): TS-Arena 61.6 over 5 weeks (rank 6) on 14.11.2026, a live benchmark where forecasts are submitted before the data exists, mostly electricity data, higher is better ([TS-Arena](https://ts-arena.live)); GIFT-Eval relative CRPS / MASE 0.452 / 0.661, recalculated from the published result files, not the official ranking, lower is better ([results](https://github.com/SalesforceAIResearch/gift-eval/tree/main/results)).
- A user running it on CPU asks for pandas DataFrame input ([tfc-t0 #19, 11.11.2026](https://github.com/theforecastingcompany/tfc-t0/issues/19)).

**Web summary** (searched 2026-11-15, feedback thin): Light alternative; quantile ordering bug on tiny-scale data fixed; benchmark leakage found by a reviewer and removed by the authors.

**Negative**

- Common to all forecasting foundation models: practitioners report they often do not beat simple baselines on their own data: TimesFM about ARIMA level while bigger and slower ([magimas, 31.03.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=47583870)); seasonal naive usually wins for e-commerce ([jc4p, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50155295)); one XGBoost model per series still wins ([niltecedu, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50156050)); XGBoost and LightGBM beat transformers on blood glucose ([articulatepang, 14.11.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50498722)). The benefit is many series without building one model each ([logancbrown, 19.10.2026](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50152506)). Backtest against a simple baseline first.
- Quantiles out of order on very small-scale data, fixed in PR #101 ([tirex-2 #81, 21.10.2026](https://github.com/NX-AI/tirex-2/issues/81)); a reviewer found GIFT-Eval solar data in the training set, the authors removed it from their reported results ([#2, 05.07.2026](https://github.com/NX-AI/tirex-2/issues/2)).

**Neutral**

- Benchmarks (leads, not user feedback): TS-Arena 58.6 over 20 weeks on 14.11.2026, a live benchmark where forecasts are submitted before the data exists, mostly electricity data, higher is better ([TS-Arena](https://ts-arena.live)); GIFT-Eval relative CRPS / MASE 0.478 / 0.697, recalculated from the published result files, not the official ranking, lower is better ([results](https://github.com/SalesforceAIResearch/gift-eval/tree/main/results)).
